Not protocols, but genuine entry points that would otherwise fall out of the checklist.

| Language | Framework | Mechanism | Shape | Tier |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `@Scheduled` — `cron`, `fixedRate(String)`, `fixedDelay(String)` | `annotation` | ✅ **shipped** (2O Task 8) — into `scheduled_work`, its own table, never `endpoints`: a method the framework calls on a clock speaks no protocol and has no address. Handler, trigger kind, expression **verbatim** |
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `@Schedules` — two or more `@Scheduled` on one method, folded by javac | `annotation` (nested) | ✅ shipped — through capability 9.16. The catalog REQUIRES that capability rather than degrading: on a scanner without it a two-schedule method would carry no readable trigger while its single-schedule neighbour carried its own, so the whole catalog is skipped **visibly** instead |
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `@Scheduled(cron = "${orders.sweep.cron}")` — a property placeholder | `annotation` | ✅ **shipped** (2026-10-23, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-23-configured-schedules-design.md`, option A). The `scheduled_work` row stays **`unresolved`, expression carried verbatim**: the schedule that runs is the deployment's, which a deployment overwrites. Beside it, `resolved_schedules` holds what THIS REPOSITORY configures — one row per (job whose expression holds a placeholder, environment of `resolved_config`), keyed by the job's `handler`, `trigger` and `expression`, substituted by the one placeholder rule (`resolve/config.py::substitute`, the config model's own delimiters, `:default` and key matching) — and the document states it under the job's row: *as configured in this repository: `0 0 * * * *` in `default`; `0 */5 * * * *` in `prod`*, or *not set in `prod` (missing_key)*. Pinned live on `fixtures/orders-service` (`scheduling/OrderSweeper`: a key set in a profile file only, beside the annotation's default, and a key set nowhere) by `tests/e2e/test_vertical_slice.py::test_a_configured_schedule_is_stated_per_environment_in_the_document`, and by the guards `schedules.a-PLACEHOLDER-expression-is-read-per-environment`, `schedules.an-ANNOTATION-default-applies-where-no-environment-sets-the-key`, `schedules.a-configured-VALUE-is-masked-on-export` and `schedules.the-DOCUMENT-states-a-schedules-configured-values`. SpEL `#{…}` is not evaluated and gets no rows (spec §4) |
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `@Scheduled(cron = "-")` — `@Scheduled.CRON_DISABLED` | `annotation` | ✅ shipped — **derives NOTHING** (`OI-2`, closed). CP-14 reads the constant off the annotation at spring-context 6.1.6, 6.2.19 and 7.0.9 and finds the post-processor comparing `cron()` against it immediately before `scheduleCronTask` and before nothing else, so the framework registers no task. `cron` is the only attribute with a sentinel: CP-14 row 3 enumerates every string comparison in that class and finds one |
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `@Scheduled(cron = "-", fixedDelay = 1000)` — a disabled cron BESIDE a live rate | `annotation` | **Boundary — OVER-reports one row (`OI-34`).** CP-14 rows 4-5: the "a schedule was declared" flag is written before the sentinel comparison and read into a failing `Assert.isTrue` after it, so the container refuses to start — while we drop the cron row and publish the `fixedDelay` one. Not encoded: every conjunct this style has is per ATTRIBUTE, and refusing the declaration as a whole is a rule the grammar does not have (§8.1 rule 4). **Falsified by** such a declaration exporting a `scheduled_work` row |
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `@Scheduled` naming two trigger attributes at once, or none — what the container does at startup with such a declaration | `annotation` | **Boundary — one row per attribute written, and none where none is.** Whether the container refuses to start is a startup question a repository cannot answer, so each declared trigger is reported as the code fact it is. A declaration naming no trigger derives nothing: there is no expression to carry |
| Java | Spring scheduling (@Scheduled) | `initialDelay`, `initialDelayString`, `zone`, `timeUnit`, `scheduler` — real `@Scheduled` attributes that are not a schedule | `annotation` | ✅ shipped — **none of them is read**, and `spring-scheduling.triggers-are-the-schedule-attributes` measures that a near-miss notices if one ever is. Reading `initialDelay` would publish the wait before the FIRST run as the interval between EVERY run |
| Java | Quartz | **Each new trigger** — `TriggerBuilder.newTrigger()…build()`, its schedule builder (`cronSchedule(…)`, `simpleSchedule()…`, `calendarIntervalSchedule()…`, `dailyTimeIntervalSchedule()…` and their static shortcuts) and the job Quartz pairs with it: `scheduleJob(job, trigger)` or `scheduleJob(job, Set, boolean)`, `forJob(name[, group])` / `forJob(JobKey)`, or `forJob(JobDetail)` from a `@Bean` | `fluent_chain` | ✅ **shipped** (2026-10-29, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-28-quartz-design.md`, catalog `quartz.yml`, engine `scheduled_chain.py`; probe CP-55, Quartz 2.5.1). **The entry comes from the trigger, never from `@DisallowConcurrentExecution`, `@PersistJobDataAfterExecution` or a job data map.** Those are concurrency and state policy, not a schedule. It is written to `scheduled_work`, one row per `newTrigger()` root (ruling 3), named `group.name` by the trigger's own key (ruling 5). The trigger kind is the schedule builder's type. A cron expression is carried verbatim, and a `${…}` placeholder gets `resolved_schedules` rows as `@Scheduled` does. A non-cron schedule is its builder chain as written (ruling 4), and javac's constants as compiled (`Calendar.TUESDAY` is `3`). The job is found wherever it is paired with the trigger, across methods and classes. The handler is the body the JVM dispatches for the job class's `execute`, or `executeInternal` under `QuartzJobBean`. Where the job class or the cron is read off a database row (RuoYi-Vue, NBlog, aurora, ruoyi-vue-pro, dolphinscheduler), the row is `partial`, with the getter that reads it named as `expression_source`; seed SQL is never read (ruling 2). Pinned by `tests/e2e/test_async_delivered.py::test_quartz_triggers_are_scheduled_work_delivered` on `fixtures/orders-quartz`. Measured on six corpora in the spec's "As built". OI-222's wrong row, a cron set on another object of the same type, was fixed 2026-10-29 in the shared value walk |
| Java | Quartz | Jobs and triggers declared in `quartz_data.xml` (the `XMLSchedulingDataProcessorPlugin`) or in `quartz.properties` | configuration file | **Boundary — derives nothing.** None of the six measured corpora schedules this way; only the Quartz library's own examples do. A Quartz module whose triggers are all in XML gets no `scheduled_work` row, and nothing says so in the document |
| Java | Quartz | Spring's `JobDetailFactoryBean` / `CronTriggerFactoryBean` / `SimpleTriggerFactoryBean` | `@Bean` factory beans | **Boundary — derives nothing.** Zero uses in all six corpora (spec §4). Stated, not built |
| Java | Quartz | Quartz in **Kotlin** | `fluent_chain` | **Boundary — unmeasured.** The bytecode is the same `TriggerBuilder` calls, but no corpus and no fixture is Kotlin, so nothing pins it |
| Java | Quartz | A job with no trigger — `newJob(…).storeDurably().build()` added with `scheduler.addJob(…)` and run on demand by `scheduler.triggerJob(…)` | `fluent_chain` | **Boundary — no entry.** It has no schedule, so it is not scheduled work; what starts it is an ordinary call in the code that calls `triggerJob`. The smoke test's `onDemandJob` is one |
| Java | Quartz | A dispatcher job that calls its target by reflection — RuoYi-Vue, aurora and NBlog look a bean and method up by a string from the jobs table and call `Method.invoke` | `fluent_chain` | **Boundary — the trace stops at the reflective call.** The entry and its handler are there; what the handler runs is named only by a database row, which is never read (ruling 2). Where the dispatcher calls through an interface instead (ruoyi-vue-pro's `JobHandler`), the trace reaches every implementation the analysed module compiles against |
| Java | Temporal / Cadence | **Temporal** — `@WorkflowInterface` + `@WorkflowMethod`, `@SignalMethod` / `@QueryMethod` / `@UpdateMethod`, `@ActivityInterface` + `@ActivityMethod` and the unannotated members of an activity contract, and `WorkflowClient.newUntypedWorkflowStub(String[, WorkflowOptions])` | `annotation` ×2 (`declared_on: interface`) + `registration_call` | ✅ **shipped** (2026-09-22, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-22-temporal-and-eventbus-design.md`). **Three catalogs, not one:** `temporal-workflow.yml` and `temporal-activity.yml` are CONTRACT catalogs — each `annotation_driven`, each `declared_on: interface`, so the declaring INTERFACE names the row (`OrderWorkflow`, never `OrderWorkflowImpl`) and the SEI fence subtracts the interfaces the module implements — plus `temporal-untyped.yml` (`single_call`) for the untyped client stub. Measured on `io.temporal:temporal-sdk` **1.27.0** and `io.temporal:temporal-spring-boot-autoconfigure` **1.27.0**, probe `scripts/probes/cp30_temporal_contract_naming.md` (`identity: assumed`: the SDK archive states no version anywhere in its own bytes — no manifest entry, no `pom.properties`, no constant — so CP-10 verifies it by SHA-1 against Maven's sidecar instead, and `1.27.0` in that column is the coordinate we asked for, not something the jar says). **Direction is declared `outbound` and flips to `inbound` only on the Spring Boot starter annotation** — `resolves.direction` keyed on an in-module class carrying `io.temporal.spring.boot.WorkflowImpl` / `ActivityImpl` — whose `taskQueues()` is the row's `endpoint_metadata` `kind: task_queue`. Signals, queries and updates are addressed `<workflow type>/<member>` (`OrderWorkflow/cancel`) through `declaring_type_scoped_name`, a fourth `path_fallback` word this spec added, with `/` stated as catalog data. **Nine endpoint rows and eight metadata rows on `fixtures/temporal-probe`**, pinned exactly by `tests/e2e/test_async_delivered.py::test_temporal_shapes_nine_rows` and `::test_temporal_shapes_eight_metadata_rows`. **Stated boundaries, in the catalogs and below:** the programmatic worker (next row); Cadence (the row after); a contract that lives in a DEPENDENCY jar rather than in the module derives nothing, Feign's and the JWS client's limitation for the same reason; a signal, query or update sent through a typed stub is an ordinary interface call on a proxy and is not read as a call site (the outbound row comes from the contract declaration instead); `newWorkflowStub(Class, workflowId)` and `newExternalWorkflowStub` address a RUNNING execution and derive nothing, as does `newUntypedWorkflowStub(WorkflowExecution, …)`; `WorkflowStub.signal` / `query` / `update` name a bare MEMBER at argument 0 with no workflow type beside it — a `cancel` that would collide with every other workflow's `cancel` — and are deliberately not declared; `setTaskQueue(String)` on `ActivityOptions` / `WorkflowOptions` / `ChildWorkflowOptions` / `ContinueAsNewOptions` (CP-30 Q8 counts four) is a queue a CALLER states at a call site and these catalogs derive from declarations, so none of the four is read; and the SDK's own start-up validations are reproduced by nothing here — duplicate workflow-type or activity names, a `@WorkflowInterface` with more than one `@WorkflowMethod`, `DynamicWorkflow`, `DynamicActivity`, `registerWorkflowImplementationFactory` (`OI-127`, **Major** — it is the one arm of this bullet that produces a WRONG-DIRECTION row rather than a missing one, and the factory form is ordinary code) and Nexus services, so a module the SDK would refuse to start still produces rows |
| Java | Temporal / Cadence | **The programmatic worker** — `WorkerFactory.newWorker("orders")` + `registerWorkflowImplementationTypes(…)` / `registerActivitiesImplementations(…)`, the registration form that carries no Spring Boot starter annotation | `registration_call` | **Boundary — derives NOTHING, in either direction.** This is the sharpest boundary in the group and it is worth stating twice: the task queue a worker actually polls is named on `WorkerFactory` at argument 0 (CP-30 Q8), and these catalogs read the queue only off `@WorkflowImpl`/`@ActivityImpl`, so a module that registers its workers in code gets no `task_queue` entry. **And because the inbound flip is keyed on that same annotation, such a module gets no inbound row for its own implementations either** — its workflows and activities are not reported as served, and its contracts fall to the SEI fence rather than becoming outbound rows, so they are neither served nor called here. That is a deliberate under-report standing in for the alternative, which is publishing an implementation nobody registered as a live endpoint. Closing it needs a mechanism that does not exist: one engine attaching metadata read at a call site to another engine's declaration-derived rows (`OI-124`) |
| Java | Temporal / Cadence | **Cadence** — `com.uber.cadence.*`, the pre-fork API with the same annotation names | `annotation` | **Boundary — derives nothing, on purpose.** Every fqn in the three catalogs is `io.temporal.*`. Cadence's annotations are spelled the same and its wire is a different cluster, so folding them under this protocol would let a Cadence worker link to a Temporal caller and be reported as answering it. Nothing here is measured against a Cadence jar. **Re-measure when** a real module carries `com.uber.cadence` (`OI-123`): the question is whether Cadence is a second protocol member or a second set of catalogs under a protocol of its own, and it is not answerable from a Temporal jar |
| Java | Vert.x EventBus | **The Vert.x event bus** — `bus.send/publish/request("addr", body[, DeliveryOptions])`, `bus.consumer/localConsumer("addr", handler)`, the two-step `bus.consumer("addr").handler(h)` and `bus.sender/publisher("addr").write(body)`, the Mutiny facade's own five call forms, and Quarkus's `@ConsumeEvent` | `registration_call` ×3 + `fluent_chain` + `annotation` | ✅ **shipped** (2026-09-22, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-22-temporal-and-eventbus-design.md`). **Four catalogs, not one:** `vertx-eventbus.yml` (`single_call`, eleven rows, every one pinned to its own erased descriptor, both major versions in the one file), `vertx-eventbus-chain.yml` (`fluent`, four roots and three steps — registration lives in the TERMINAL, `handler` or `write`, measured at offsets 9 and 15 of `EventBusImpl.consumer`, so a consumer built and never given a handler derives nothing), `vertx-eventbus-mutiny.yml` (`single_call`, eight rows on `io.vertx.mutiny.core.eventbus.EventBus` — its own file because that type shares **not one** erased descriptor with the core interface and a `single_call` catalog's `owner` is catalog-level), and `quarkus-consume-event.yml` (`annotation_driven`, `@ConsumeEvent`, a bare use addressing the FULLY-QUALIFIED declaring class name exactly as `VertxProcessor` does, and `local()` recorded as `endpoint_metadata` `kind: local` carrying the declaration's own boolean with `absent_value: "true"` measured off the annotation's `AnnotationDefault`). Measured on `io.vertx:vertx-core` **4.5.10 and 5.0.0** separately, `io.smallrye.reactive:smallrye-mutiny-vertx-core` **3.16.0**, `io.quarkus:quarkus-vertx` and `quarkus-vertx-deployment` **3.17.5**, probe `scripts/probes/cp31_eventbus_surface.md`, every version read from the archive's own `Implementation-Version` (`identity: reported`). **20 endpoint rows and 8 metadata rows on `fixtures/orders-eventbus`**, pinned exactly by `tests/e2e/test_async_delivered.py::test_eventbus_shapes_rows` (the 20, seven columns each) and `::test_eventbus_local_metadata_is_the_measured_default_or_the_declared_value` (the 8 — an exact set, so a `metadata_from` writing its `absent_value` unconditionally still produces seven of them and `orders.cluster`'s `false` is what fails it). **The bus's own envelope is a declared wrapper on all four files** (2026-09-22 fix): `@ConsumeEvent` permits a `Message<T>` parameter outright — CP-31 Q12 reads `VertxProcessor`'s own constant, *"must return void"* is the only condition it attaches — so `h(Message<Order>)` and `h(Order)` are two spellings of ONE contract and recorded two shapes until `Message` was declared `semantics: envelope` (`spring-kafka.yml`'s spelling for `ConsumerRecord`, the same thing one bus over). `payload_arg: 0` is `body()`, the one descriptor of the envelope's five that names what crossed the wire; the other four — `address()`, `headers()`, `replyAddress()`, `isSend()` — are framing the bus adds and strips (CP-31 Q10a/Q10b, byte-identical at 4.5.10 and 5.0.0). **The core and Mutiny `Message` are DIFFERENT types** (Q10c) and both are declared on the Quarkus catalog because `VertxConstants.isMessage` accepts either (Q11) — as does `isMessageHeaders`, which is why `request_body.excludes` names `io.vertx.core.MultiMap` AND `io.vertx.mutiny.core.MultiMap`; naming only the core one left the Mutiny headers/body split deriving no payload at all, and the Mutiny spelling is the mainstream one in a Quarkus service. `test_the_envelope_spelling_and_the_payload_spelling_record_one_shape` pins both envelope spellings against their plain sibling as an EQUALITY. **Stated boundaries:** the envelope wrapper is declared on the three programmatic catalogs (`vertx-eventbus.yml`, `-chain`, `-mutiny`) for AGREEMENT alone and no fixture arm exercises it there — shape expansion is a global insert-if-absent pass keyed on the type symbol, so a file left without the rule could reach a `Message` first and decide the answer for every catalog after it (measured by running the analysis with the entries removed). **What a forwarded envelope — `bus.send("out", msg)` — actually puts on the wire is UNMEASURED**: `send` hands the object to a codec and no probe here asks whether the `Message` or its body crosses, so those three entries make no claim about that call and the question is CP-31's to answer if such a call site turns up. `request(...)` derives **no `response_shape`** (the next row); a consumer or producer handed across methods (the row after); `Message.reply(...)` — the reply goes to a reply-to address the SENDER generated, which no declaration in the module names; the SockJS/TCP bridge, whose `PermittedOptions().setAddressRegex(…)` is a POLICY and a regex is not an address; Vert.x **3.x**, unmeasured and matched by no descriptor here; the 5.0 options-builder tail `new MessageConsumerOptions().setAddress("a").setLocalOnly(true)`, which reaches `consumer(...)` as the result of the LAST setter so `channel_via` finds `setLocalOnly` and the address reads `unresolved` (CP-31 Q5b measured that `setLocalOnly` and `setMaxBufferedMessages` really do return the options object, so the tail is reachable in real code — `setOrdered`, the spec's own example, does not exist); `bodyStream().handler(h)`, whose `handler` is declared on `ReadStream` and is owner-gated out deliberately; the `DeliveryOptions` producer overloads and the 5.0 options-rooted chain, declared by the jars and not made roots; and, on the Quarkus catalog, a `${…}` address carried verbatim at `unresolved`, no reply row (the framework has no `@SendTo` equivalent, and a reply row naming the consumer's own address would claim the endpoint answers itself), the failure-code constants, and Quarkus other than 3.17.5 |
| Java | Vert.x EventBus | `request(addr, body)` — the request/reply call, and the SHAPE of the reply it gets back | `registration_call` | **Boundary — the request body derives, the reply shape does not.** `response_shape` is NULL on every event-bus row, `orders.lookup` included. The reply type is the CALL'S RETURN TYPE — `Future<Message<T>>` on the core interface, `Uni<Message<T>>` on the Mutiny facade — and `single_call`'s only response word is `response_arg`, an argument POSITION. No argument of a `request` call carries the reply type: there is no `Class` literal and no `ParameterizedTypeReference`, so nothing the engine can read names it, and reading argument 1 would publish the REQUEST's shape as the reply's. Closing it is an engine word for "the declared return type" (`OI-125`). **This boundary retires deliberately rather than quietly:** `test_every_eventbus_row_is_verbless_and_states_no_reply_shape` asserts `response_shape is None` over every row, so the day that word lands the test FAILS and somebody has to come back to this line |
| Java | Vert.x EventBus | A `MessageConsumer` or `MessageProducer` assigned to a local and handed to another method before its terminal — `MessageConsumer<T> c = bus.consumer("a"); register(c);` | `fluent_chain` | **Boundary — derives nothing.** The chain walk is per method body (value ids are per method, and a cross-body `source` read is a fabrication path), so the `handler` call inside `register` is never joined to the root that named the address, and no row is written for either half. The same cross-method limit `OI-110` states for a Spring Integration container built two beans away, one framework over (`OI-126`). A stated miss, never a wrong address |
| Java | Spring Integration | Spring Integration `@ServiceActivator` (`inputChannel`, `outputChannel`) and `@InboundChannelAdapter` (`channel`) — the annotation names a CHANNEL, and configuration — where a project has any — says what the channel means | `config_join` | ✅ **shipped as a hand-off** (2O Task 9), through `config_join`'s new `binding.from: annotation_value`. Spring Integration defines no standard configuration key for a channel's destination — real apps wire them in bean definitions, and any key template would be a PROJECT convention — so the built-in declares no `key`, derives **no endpoint row and no ghost row**, and files one `channel_destination` open question per channel carrying the channel name and the handler symbol. Never a fabricated wire, never `peer: in-process`. **Since 2026-09-21 the question is asked only for a channel nothing FEEDS**: this catalog writes the `read_by` end of the channel edge (`channel_bindings`, a `${…}` name resolved through configuration first), the three broker catalogs below write the `fed_by` end, and a channel with both is a row, not a question. Because the bean fold's name rung reads those channel names in the same engine loop, **this catalog runs FIRST** — a `config_join` file whose whole effect is the edge's read end (annotation-valued binding, no `key`, no media block) has nothing to run after; a channels catalog that also declared a `key` would keep the style's last turn, a stated boundary with no shipped instance |
| Java | Spring Integration | The DESTINATION behind a channel — `Amqp.inboundAdapter(cf, String…)`, `setQueueNames(String…)`, `Kafka.messageDrivenChannelAdapter(cf, String…)`, `new PubSubInboundChannelAdapter(ops, "sub")` — the queue, topic or subscription the adapter feeding that channel actually reads, in BOTH spellings: the bean-wired registration (`@Bean` methods constructing an adapter, a container, a handler) and the Java DSL flow (`IntegrationFlow.from(<source spec>)…handle(…)`) | `fluent_chain` | ✅ **shipped for AMQP, Kafka and Pub/Sub** (2026-09-21, spec 2026-09-21 §3–§6; `spring-integration-amqp.yml`, `spring-integration-kafka.yml`, `spring-integration-pubsub.yml` — the rows in §5.9, §5.8 and §5.11 carry each broker's forms, measurements and boundaries). Was: **Boundary — readable and unread.** Since extractor capability 9.15 these arrive as `{"kind":"array","elements":[…]}` (`scripts/probes/spring-integration-destinations.md`), call-site arguments `config_join`'s configuration-file join could not reach. The `fluent` engine now walks the registration — a constructor root and its void setters, or a source spec continuing into the flow — captures the destination through the field ladder (one row per varargs element), resolves a bean argument through `resolve/beans.py`, and writes the channel it feeds as a `fed_by` edge; `resolve/channel_bindings.py` joins that to the channel index's `read_by` and mints the row naming the wire, the handler and the constructing method. Pinned by `tests/e2e/test_async_delivered.py` (`test_a_fed_channel_is_a_row_not_a_question`, `test_the_remaining_questions_are_exactly_the_unfed_channels`, the three per-broker row tests) |
| Java | Spring Integration | `@Transformer`, `@Router`, `@Filter`, `@Splitter`, `@Aggregator`, `@BridgeFrom`/`@BridgeTo` — the rest of the endpoint-annotation family — each also carries `inputChannel`/`outputChannel` | `config_join` | **Boundary — asks nothing.** Only the two annotations the probe measured are declared; §8.1 requires a framework's rule to be measured before it is built on, and the probe's harness carries neither. Each is one `channels` row once measured, with no engine change |
| Java | Spring Integration | `@InboundChannelAdapter("orders")` — the `value` spelling of `channel` — the attribute written positionally | `config_join` | **Boundary — asks nothing.** The probe measured the `channel` spelling only, and that the two are `@AliasFor` each other is a fact of a jar this analysis does not scan. Closes with one measured `channels` row |
| Java | Spring Integration | Spring Integration Java DSL flows — `IntegrationFlow.from(<source spec>)`, the flow's members (`transform`, `filter`, `channel`, `handle`, `route`, …), its consumer, and a flow sourced from a channel (`from("ch")`). The flow definition's 34 public members (CP-27 row 2; `IntegrationFlows` does not exist at 6.2.4, row 1) are classified once in `catalogs/step-models/spring-integration.yml` — pass-through, channel link, handler, discard (`nullChannel`), divert (`route`, `gateway`, `scatterGather`, `publishSubscribeChannel`, `controlBus`, `trigger`, `to` — each constructs a router or fan-out, row 4) — and merged into each broker catalog, on the owners javac names at the call sites (row 16) | `fluent_chain` | ✅ **shipped** (2026-09-21, spec 2026-09-21 §5). A source spec's chain continues into `from(…)` and along the flow to `handle(lambda)` — the lambda body is the row's `handler` — or to `.channel("x")`, which is the channel the flow feeds (the join mints the row against `x`'s readers); `handle(h).channel("y")` keeps `h` and records `y` as its output (`sends_to`, no reader yet — `OI-107`); a `divert` before `handle` leaves `handler` NULL with the row resolved (the message may leave the linear flow — never a guessed consumer); `from("ch").handle(lambda)` makes the flow `ch`'s READER (`from("ch").channel("b").handle(lambda)` makes it `b`'s — the handler reads the channel linked before it) and `from("ch").handle(<sink spec>)` the outbound row with `ch` fed outbound; `handleReactive` ends a flow without `get()` and is a terminal on every broker catalog. **Stated boundaries:** the lambda flow form's implicit `<beanName>.input` channel (its `handle(sink)` DOES derive, `caller` the lambda body); `from(Supplier)`/`from(MessageSource)`; `from(<adapter bean>)` (`OI-112`); a bridge (`from("a").channel("b")`, neither wire nor handler — nothing); `channel(MessageChannelSpec)`/`channel(Function)` (the link unreadable from there on); `handle(bean, "method")` where the bean is not the module's; `.channel("x").handle(h).channel("y")` loses `fed_by(x)` (`OI-115`); two-hop container sources (`OI-110`). The broker-specific boundaries — Kafka's `Pattern`/`TopicPartitionOffset` subscriptions and AMQP's `configureContainer(c -> c.addQueueNames(…))` — are stated on the broker rows, §5.8's "Spring Integration Kafka (DSL + adapters)" and §5.9's "Spring Integration AMQP (adapters + DSL)". Pinned by `tests/e2e/test_async_delivered.py::test_kafka_shapes_rows`, `test_amqp_shapes_rows` |
| Java | Spring Integration | XML-configured flows — the flow's topology, and the anonymous channels inside it | `filesystem` | **Boundary — derives nothing.** An XML-configured flow contributes **zero** facts — the extractor reads `target/classes/**/*.class` and the XML is a resource sitting beside them — so it needs a resource reader of its own, the same category as the `.avsc` reader |
| Java | Spring Integration | The `protocol` column on a `channel_destination` question — which broker, if any, a channel actually reaches | `config_join` | **Boundary — the column is EMPTY, and that is the answer.** It read `amqp` from Task 9 until OI-1's fix: a wire STYLE had to declare a protocol (`resolve/endpoints.py`) and D00's closed set has no "unknown" member (NFR-5), so `spring-integration.yml` declared the one value the probe measured a channel join against end to end (Q3) — and **every** question it filed was filed under it, a Kafka- or JMS-fed channel's included. The rule is now keyed on whether the CATALOG derives endpoint rows rather than on what its style is nominally called, so a catalog that mints none declares no wire identity and its questions carry none. What remains is an ABSENCE, stated as one: nothing here reads which broker feeds a channel, the ledger row says so, and the renderer prints the channel name alone. **Since 2026-09-21 the protocol DOES come from the adapter that feeds the channel** — for a channel some `@Bean` adapter or DSL flow feeds, the `fed_by` edge carries the broker catalog's protocol and the join mints a row under it, and no question is filed at all; the column is empty only on the questions that remain, which are exactly the channels nothing in the module feeds. **Falsified by** a question carrying a protocol nothing measured |
| Java | Akka / Pekko actors | **Akka / Pekko actor messaging** `ref ! msg`, `ref.tell(msg)` — receiver comes from an `ActorRef` created elsewhere | — | **C, possibly permanent — see §7.** *Tier unchanged by the value walk, and now measured rather than assumed (2026-10-17): spec 2026-09-22 §4 claimed its sixth edge would close this row, and `scripts/probes/cp33_binding_writes.md` row 7 shows it would not. `ActorRef ref = system.actorOf(props, "worker")` assigns a CALL'S RESULT — `aload_0; aload_1; iconst_0; invokeinterface; checkcast; putfield` — so the `ALOAD 0; xLOAD k; PUTFIELD` triple a `field_write` fact needs never presents. A setter-injected reference would be read; the factory form the framework documents would not. `OI-136`* |
| Python | Celery | **Celery** `@app.task`, `task.delay(…)`, `.apply_async(queue=…)` — a real async boundary, easy to miss | `annotation` | A |
| TS | bullmq | `bullmq` `new Queue("n")`, `new Worker("n", fn)` — string names, matchable like topics | `registration_call` | A |
| Go | robfig/cron, Temporal Go SDK | `robfig/cron` `c.AddFunc("@hourly", fn)`; Temporal Go SDK `w.RegisterWorkflow(fn)` | `registration_call` | A |
| Go | asynq, machinery | `asynq`, `machinery` task queues — `mux.HandleFunc("task:name", h)` | `registration_call` | A |
| Rust | tokio-cron-scheduler | `tokio-cron-scheduler` `Job::new("0 * * * * *", fn)` | `registration_call` | A |
| Rust | Temporal Rust SDK | Temporal Rust SDK — `register_workflow::<T>()`, `register_activities()`; **public preview, API still evolving** | `registration_call` | A |
| Rust | apalis | `apalis` — worker pulls from a Postgres/Redis/SQLite backend | `registration_call` | B |
| Python | APScheduler | APScheduler `@scheduler.scheduled_job("cron", …)` | `annotation` | A |
| Swift | BGTaskScheduler | `BGTaskScheduler.register(forTaskWithIdentifier:)` — identifiers also declared in `Info.plist` (§6) | `registration_call` | B |
| Any | — | Database access — Room `@Query`, JPA repositories, ORM models — a data boundary, not a protocol | `annotation` | B |

### 5.13 Language construct

Not a protocol, and not a library: a thing the COMPILER emits that a reader of the class
file has to recognise. **Almost nothing in this section is measured against real Kotlin**: the six corpora under
`examples/` contain **zero `.kt` files** between them, and the fixture contains exactly TWO —
`KotlinOrder.kt`, a data class, and `KotlinOrderRoutes.kt`, a reactive `coRouter`.

**Each row below says for itself whether the fixture exercises it**, and that is deliberate:
this paragraph carried a summary sentence for four revisions and was wrong every time — "no
`.kt` files at all", then "only nullability", then "nullability and `suspend fun`", then
"exactly one". A count that has to be re-derived whenever the fixture gains a class does not
belong in a preamble; the fact belongs beside the claim it qualifies. That is the honest state and it is why the levels are what they are. Kotlin is where this axis earns its keep — §5.1 to §5.12 hold **0
Kotlin rows out of 186** while `coverage_matrix.py` aliases Kotlin to Java, so the matrix
has been asserting a parity nobody measured.

| Construct | What the class file holds | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| Kotlin property nullability | The compiler states it once, in the protobuf blob inside every Kotlin class's `@kotlin.Metadata` — not in any annotation a scanner could read the way JSpecify's `@Nullable` is read | Scanner capability `kotlin_metadata` (M03) | **supported** — `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinNullability.java` decodes the blob and maps each property to the nullability its declared type carries — except a property declared as a bare type variable (`class Box<T>(val x: T)`), which is left to Java's rule on all three projections, field, getter and parameter, and whose field carries no `ext.kotlin` block at all (2026-09-12, `KotlinParameterNullabilityTest`) **Live at the FACT level and nowhere else**: the fixture's `KotlinOrder#id` carries `{"kotlin": {"nullable": false}}` and `#note` carries `true`, so the decoder fires here rather than only in `extractor-jvm`'s tests — but `KotlinOrder` is used by nothing, reaches no wire boundary, and contributes none of the fixture's 54 `shape_fields`. **The GETTER half closed 2026-09-11** (`G-63`): `getId()` reported `nullable: true, nullable_resolution: "resolved"` beside the `@NotNull` kotlinc writes on the same method — a certainty marker on a value the same fact contradicted — and now reports `false`, while `getNote()` correctly keeps `true`. **The PARAMETER half closed 2026-09-12** (`OI-82`, `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinParameterNullabilityTest.java`): a function's and a constructor's value parameters carry the nullability their declared type states, at every level of the type — `place(sku: String, qty: Int?, tags: List<String?>, meta: Map<String, List<Int>?>?)` reads `false, true, false[true], true[false, true[false]]`, and `KotlinOrder#<init>`'s `(id: String, note: String?)` reads `false, true` where both read `true` before. Metadata's type tree is walked beside the reference built from `Signature`, and applied only where the two agree on the shape at every level; where they do not — an inner class's enclosing instance, an enum's name/ordinal, both of which metadata's parameter list omits, and a `var` property's setter, whose descriptor metadata never keys at all — every parameter of that method keeps Java's rule (an extension function's receiver is omitted the same way, and since `kotlin-extension-functions` the declared types are laid from the slot after it — the extension-functions row below), which is less specific and never wrong (a `$default` bridge's descriptor is one metadata never keys either, and since `OI-81` it is read under its sibling's key instead — the row below). **A bare type variable keeps Java's rule too** (2026-09-12, with `OI-80`): metadata's "not nullable" on `x: T` in `fun <T> ranked(x: T): T` describes the variable, whose implicit bound is `Any?`, not what a caller binds it to, so `x: T` and the return `T` keep Java's `nullable: true` rather than metadata's `false`, while `y: T?` reads `true` because it is; the FIELD of a `val x: T` is left the same way and carries no `ext.kotlin` block, so the field, the getter and the constructor parameter of one declared type agree; a bound that would settle it (`T : Any`) is not read. |
| Kotlin `suspend fun` | An extra trailing `Continuation` parameter and an erased `Object` return | Q2's Kotlin boundaries; `REQUIREMENTS` scope line | **supported** (`G-63`, 2026-09-11). A suspending function is read as the ordinary function it describes: the trailing synthetic is dropped and the return is recovered from that synthetic's own type argument in `Signature`, with `kotlin/Unit` becoming `void` as a non-suspending `fun` returning `Unit` already reads. Nothing downstream knows coroutines exist, and no identity moved — a symbol hashes the RAW descriptor. **The type comes from `Signature`, the nullability from `@kotlin.Metadata`, and neither could supply the other**: measured, `fetch(String): KotlinSample` and `maybe(String): KotlinSample?` compile to one descriptor and one signature hash with kotlinc stamping `@Nullable` on both, so only metadata separates them; metadata in turn spells Kotlin classifiers whose boxed-versus-primitive mapping depends on the nullability itself. **Whether a method is suspending is metadata's statement, never its shape** — the structural test fires on 683 of 14,895 methods across `kotlinx-coroutines-core-jvm` 1.8.0 and `kotlin-stdlib` 2.2.0, and 228 of those are not suspend functions, mostly the `<init>` of generated state-machine classes. **Live on the fixture**: `KotlinOrderRoutes#listOrders` published `[request, $completion]` returning `java/lang/Object#` and now publishes `[request]` returning `ServerResponse#`, non-null. The endpoint row still carries no handler (§2), which is a separate gap. **At parity on primitives since 2026-09-12** (`OI-80`, `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinSuspendPrimitiveTest.java`): a type argument cannot be a primitive, so the `Continuation` spelled `suspend fun suspCount(): Int` as `Integer` where `fun plainCount(): Int`'s descriptor says `I`. Metadata's declared classifier, read beside the nullability it already supplied, now maps the eight `kotlin/Int`-style classifiers to the primitive when declared non-null — `suspCount` reads `int#`, the same whole reference as `plainCount` — and leaves `suspMaybeCount(): Int?` as `java/lang/Integer#`, nullable, because a nullable `Int` genuinely boxes. |
| Kotlin extension functions | A static method whose FIRST parameter is the receiver | Q2's Kotlin boundaries | **supported** — shipped 2026-09-12 as `kotlin-extension-functions`, proved by `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinExtensionsTest.java`. Measured on kotlinc 2.2.0: a top-level `fun String.shout()` compiles into the file facade `KotlinExtensionsSampleKt` (`@kotlin.Metadata` `k=2`) as `static shout(Ljava/lang/String;)`, a member `fun List<Int>.total()` as the instance method `total(Ljava/util/List;)`, and a suspending extension keeps the receiver first with the `Continuation` last — descriptor slot 0 in every case, named only in the `LocalVariableTable` (`$this$shout`), with no `MethodParameters` attribute. `KotlinNullability` now decodes the file facade (it returned `NONE` for anything but a class, so a top-level function's return and parameters took Java's rule and a top-level `suspend fun` kept its `Continuation`; both now read as a member's do) and keeps the key of every function whose metadata carries a receiver parameter type; the scanner emits `receiver: true` on that slot (absent means false) and lays the declared value parameters from the slot after it, so `fun String.repeatShout(suffix: String)`'s `suffix` reads `nullable: false` where it took Java's rule before. The slot is kept, not dropped — a call site names the method by its full descriptor — and its own type keeps Java's rule; the `$default` bridge of an extension with defaults (`decorate$default(Ljava/lang/String;Ljava/lang/String;ILjava/lang/Object;)`, receiver first, no owner) marks the same slot through `OI-81`'s sibling read. **Not read**: extension PROPERTIES' getters (`val String.first: Char`), top-level properties, and `@JvmMultifileClass` parts (`KotlinClassMetadata.MultiFileClassPart` still decodes to `NONE`) — their receivers are unmarked and their types keep Java's rule. No corpus here has one on a wire boundary, so there is still no live instance |
| Kotlin `$default` synthetic methods | The compiler emits a second method carrying the default-argument mask beside every function with default parameters | Q2's Kotlin boundaries | **shipped** (`OI-81`, 2026-09-12, `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinDefaultBridgeTest.java`). The bridge is recognised from its shape — measured on kotlinc 2.2.0, `greet$default` is `static synthetic (Owner;Ljava/lang/String;ZILjava/lang/Object;)Ljava/lang/String;`: the receiver (absent for a `@JvmStatic` function), the function's own slots, one `int` mask per 32 parameters, an `Object` marker — and its fact carries `bridge_of: "greet"` beside `synthetic: true`, with its return and the function's own slots reading the types the FUNCTION declared: `name: String` reads `nullable: false` where it read Java's `true` beside a `greet` reading `false`. The receiver, the masks, the marker and a suspend sibling's `Continuation` keep Java's rule. **A suspend sibling's bridge returns the erased `java/lang/Object#`** with the declared nullability laid over it — accurate for the erased protocol (the value or the `COROUTINE_SUSPENDED` marker), not the declared type, which only the sibling's `Signature` holds and the bridge does not. **Where the receiver question cannot be settled** — metadata keys neither candidate sibling, or keys both (an `object`'s `@JvmStatic fun js(a: String, b: Int = 0)` beside an arity overload `fun js(b: Int)`) — the bridge carries `bridge_of` and nothing else: ambiguity is not a guess, so every slot and the return keep Java's rule. **Still two symbols**, on purpose: a call site names the bridge by its own descriptor, and every consumer that filters `synthetic: true` already excludes it from a wire boundary, so `bridge_of` is what dispatch follows back. **It moves rows as of spec 2026-09-22 §1a**, which was not true when this cell was written: `call_edges`, `return_flows` and `method_resolution` are built over `effective_call_sites`, so a call the compiler wrote against the bridge is recorded against the sibling — measured on `fixtures/orders-ktor`, whose one `mount$default` site moves all three. The bridge carries **no signature at all** (`GenericSignatureTest`), so its parameterized slots stay erased at the type level — only the declared nullability is borrowed, at the levels the erased reference and metadata agree on, which for a `List<String?>` is the top. **Live**: a data class emits one, and the fixture's `KotlinOrder#copy$default` now reads `bridge_of: "copy"`. |
| Kotlin `$default` bridge CALLS | A call that omits a defaulted argument targets the bridge — a different name and arity from the function — with one `int` mask per 32 declared parameters (a set bit = the caller omitted that parameter) and a trailing `Object` marker | Spec 2026-09-19 §3a (`kotlin-default-bridge-calls`) | **shipped** (2026-09-20, `resolve/effective_calls.py`, `tests/application/test_effective_calls.py`). Before any engine reads call sites, a site whose target is a declared bridge (`bridge_of`, above) is re-targeted to its sibling: the mask and marker slots drop, each parameter the mask marks omitted becomes `{kind: unresolved, reason: defaulted}`, the receiver slot becomes the row's receiver for an instance sibling, and `evidence_bridge` keeps the bridge target so a reader can see the call WAS one. Mask bits index the DECLARED parameters — an extension receiver (`receiver: true`, the sibling's slot 0) is not one, measured on `fixtures/orders-ktor`'s `mount$default` (mask 2 = slot 2). Left alone, stated: a bridge with no `bridge_of` in the facts, both receiver readings naming a declared sibling, a mask that is not an `int` literal, constructor defaults (`DefaultConstructorMarker`, no bridge name). The raw `call_sites` facts are untouched. **Live**: `fixtures/orders-ktor`'s `mount("/d") { }` reaches the engine as `mount` with `version` unresolved (`tests/application/test_ktor_defaults_fixture.py`). The receiver mark comes from `@Metadata`, so this holds only for Kotlin the extractor reads (§3a): before OI-219, on kotlinc 2.4 output, the mask was laid from slot 0 and `mount("/d")` marked the PATH defaulted. `orders-ktor` is built with Kotlin 2.4.20 since OI-219, and the pin holds on it |
| Kotlin lambda CLASSES (`lambda_body`) | A lambda compiled as a class — every `suspend` lambda in both modes, every lambda under `-Xlambdas=class` — extends `kotlin.jvm.internal.Lambda` or `kotlin.coroutines.jvm.internal.SuspendLambda`; a function reference `::f` extends `FunctionReferenceImpl` (or `AdaptedFunctionReference` when adapted); the author's statements are in `invokeSuspend(Object)` for a suspend lambda, else the one non-synthetic `invoke` | Spec 2026-09-19 §3b, R2 (`kotlin-class-lambdas`, `kotlin-null-handler`) | **shipped** (2026-09-20, `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinLambdaBodyTest.java`, CP-23 Q1–Q7, Q9). The class's TYPE fact carries `lambda_body: "<body method's bare symbol>"` (D00 §5, beside `bridge_of`), and `resolve/effective_calls.py` reads an `instance` argument of such a class — or the `enum`-kind `GETSTATIC …$1.INSTANCE` singleton of a non-capturing one — as `{kind: lambda, symbol: <body>, captures: 0}`, so `handler_arg`/`body_arg` and every engine see the lambda they were written for. Measured on kotlinc 2.2.0: plain lambdas are `invokedynamic` by default, classes under `-Xlambdas=class`; suspend lambdas are classes in BOTH modes, which is why every Kotlin route handler was NULL. `AdaptedFunctionReference` is recognised by name and unmeasured (`OI-103`). **Live**: `orders-ktor` 9 marked classes, 28 of 28 rows naming their handler; `orders-service` 3 of 3 Kotlin router rows; the `-Xlambdas=class` twin derives byte-identical rows |
| Kotlin parameter defaults (`declares_default`) | `@kotlin.Metadata`'s `declaresDefaultValue` flag on a value parameter — the default's EXPRESSION lives in the `$default` bridge's body, never on the declaration | Spec 2026-09-19 §3c, R1 | **shipped** (2026-09-20, `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinNullability.java`, CP-23 Q8). The parameter fact carries `declares_default: true`, absent otherwise (D00 §6, a shared field beside the nullability projection, never `ext.kotlin`: a binder rule compares it across languages). Its one consumer is `request_params`' `binder` rule — Spring's `KotlinDelegate.isOptional` treats a defaulted parameter as optional, and so does this (`@RequestParam page: Int = 0` → `optional`, `KotlinParamController.kt`, pinned in `test_a_kotlin_defaulted_or_nullable_parameter_is_optional_to_the_binder`) |
| Kotlin `DefaultImpls` | An interface with a default method body compiles to a nested `DefaultImpls` class holding the body | Q2's Kotlin boundaries | **supported** — shipped 2026-09-12 as `kotlin-defaultimpls`, proved by `extractor-jvm/.../KotlinDefaultImplsTest.java` (the facts) and `tests/application/test_call_edges.py`'s `default_impls` cases (the dispatch). Measured on kotlinc 2.2.0 with no `-jvm-default` flag: `Pricer$DefaultImpls` is `public final` — NOT `synthetic` — with `@kotlin.Metadata k=3`, no fields, no constructor, and every method `public static` with the interface at descriptor slot 0 (`price(LPricer;)I`, `discount(LPricer;II)I`, and the `static synthetic discount$default(LPricer;IIILjava/lang/Object;)I` bridge), plus an `InnerClasses` entry naming the interface as its outer class; under `-jvm-default=disable` (kotlinc 2.4.10) the holder's flags, methods and descriptors are identical. The scanner recognises the holder from all three of its name, that `InnerClasses` entry and every method having that shape (a hand-written nested class of the name fails at its own constructor), writes `implements_default_of: "<Iface>#"` on the holder's type fact — module-prefixed as `enclosing` is — and `bridge_of: "<interface method>"` on each body, the `$default` bridge inside it naming the function as it does anywhere. The holder's own metadata declares no function, so a body's slots and return keep Java's rule; the declared types are in the interface's class file, and the scanner reads one class at a time. **Dispatch** (`resolve/dispatch.py`, read by `call_edges` and `return_flows`): a call to an interface method that declares no body of its own resolves to the holder body the scanner attributed to it, matched by name and erased parameter types with slot 0 dropped — and ONLY where the interface declares none, because kotlinc 2.2.0's default mode emits BOTH a JVM default method on the interface and a `@Deprecated` holder stub calling back into it, and joining the stub too would turn every such call from `resolved` to `narrowed`. **What this closes is narrower than this row used to read**: in both modes kotlinc also gives every implementing class its own forwarding `price()` (`invokespecial Pricer.price()` under `enable`, `invokestatic Pricer$DefaultImpls.price(this)` under `disable`), so a scanned implementor already reached the body in two hops through ordinary call edges; the join closes the case where the implementors are outside the scanned module and the holder body was the boundary. **The consequence for a SCANNED implementor under `disable`**: its forwarding method is already a candidate, so the holder body joining beside it moves such a call from `resolved` (1 candidate) to `narrowed` (2) — parity with what `enable` mode already reads (the interface's default plus the forwarder), and the JVM never dispatches to the holder directly. **Not live**: no interface in the estate declares a default method body. |
| Nested and inner classes | Ordinary class files with `InnerClasses` naming their enclosure | Phase 2C | **supported** — the phase-1 skip is lifted; annotations resolve the same nested or not **Live**: 594 nested-class symbols across the estate. |
| Lambda bodies | `invokedynamic` plus a synthetic method holding the body | Scanner capability `lambda_body` (2Q, M03 9.17) | **supported** — a call made inside a lambda is an edge from the enclosing method **Live**: 15 lambda-body symbols, and FOUR endpoints are handled by one — `GET /other`, `GET`/`HEAD /fn/health`, and a Pub/Sub subscription. |
| Formal type parameters | `Signature` (JVMS 4.7.9), which the erased descriptor discards | Scanner capability `type_parameters` (W5) | **supported** — see §5.14's substitution row for what is done with them |
| Throw exits | An `ATHROW` instruction: the method leaves through it, and no method is called | `G-52` (F1 §2.3) | **supported** — shipped 2026-09-12. Every `ATHROW` is a `call_site` fact with `invoke_kind: "throw"` whose target is the thrown TYPE and no method (`name` and `descriptor` absent, D00 §5), so a trace that ends in a throw stops there and says so instead of looking like one that fell off the end. The type is read from the thrown value's producer — a `NEW` names it exactly, an invoke's declared return type is a bound the class file states — and is NULL for a rethrow, whose type lives in the try-catch table this pass does not read; the exit is published either way, because the exit is the fact and the type is the detail. `resolve/call_edges` gives the site a row with no candidates and `terminal_kind: throws`, decided from the instruction rather than by a catalog rule (the one member of `TerminalKind` produced that way — `OI-75`). Proved by `extractor-jvm/.../MethodFlowTest.java`'s `publishes_a_throw_as_an_exit_naming_the_thrown_type_and_no_method` (the three producers), `tests/domain/test_facts.py` (the shape and the invariant that only a throw names no method, neither half of one), `tests/application/test_terminals.py`'s `test_a_throw_edge_is_throws_from_its_invoke_kind_and_not_from_a_rule` and `tests/application/test_call_edges.py`'s `test_a_throw_site_is_one_resolved_edge_to_the_thrown_type_and_reads_throws` (the row: no callee, `resolved`, `throws`, NULL target for a rethrow), `tests/application/test_trace.py`'s `test_a_throw_with_no_target_stays_null_on_its_step` and `tests/application/test_reachability.py`'s `test_a_throw_whose_type_was_unread_reaches_no_symbol` (the NULL stays NULL and names no symbol), `tests/egress/test_render_traces.py`'s `test_a_throw_step_is_an_arrow_labelled_throw_into_the_thrown_type` and `test_a_throw_whose_type_was_unread_is_a_self_arrow_said_in_words` (the diagram arrow reads `throw` into the type's lifeline; a type-unread throw is a self-arrow on the method that exits, labelled in words, and the step table's call cell reads `throws (type unread)`) and `tests/e2e/test_vertical_slice.py` (the counts). **Live**: 39 throw sites on the fixture — 8 named types, 5 rethrows unread — and all 39 read `throws`. **`signature.throws` — the DECLARED clause beside this row's OBSERVED exits — is consumed by the document, `Fails with`, shipped 2026-09-12 (`G-74`).** The endpoint detail block's `- Fails with:` line pairs the handler's own declared exceptions (`handler_signature`, read the same way the Request/Response lines already do) with a reachable throw-exit call site (`reachable_throws`, joining `reachability` to `call_sites` at the composition root) — declared first, both sorted, an unread rethrow's target said in words once rather than repeated. Proved by `tests/egress/test_render.py`'s `test_a_handlers_declared_exception_renders_as_fails_with`, `test_a_reachable_throw_exit_joins_the_same_line_after_the_declared_half`, `test_a_throw_site_whose_target_was_unread_says_so_in_words` and `test_no_fails_with_line_where_neither_half_has_anything`. |

### 5.14 Shape and contract

The payload axis. A row here is a SHAPE question — what a field is called on the wire, what
a wrapper does to the body — and none of it is per-protocol: the same wrapper behaves the
same way over REST and over a message broker.

| Surface | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| Generic type-parameter substitution | A declaration says `CommonResult<T>` and a use says `CommonResult<OrderView>`; the fields of the use are the declaration's with `T` replaced | Implied by FR-4; one adjacent sentence at `COVERAGE.md` about Data REST only | **supported** (W25), for a TYPE's own parameters. The map is built POSITIONALLY — a reference's arguments are positional, so the first declared name binds the first argument — and a reference carrying fewer arguments than the declaration has parameters binds nothing rather than binding what it can, because a partial binding reads as a real type downstream. A generic METHOD's own parameters are deliberately excluded from the map's keys: a class and a method that both declare `T` are two unrelated scopes, and substituting across them is the hazard, not the feature |
| `Page<T>` / `Slice<T>` | **Interfaces**, so they declare no fields — and Spring does NOT send the element type on the wire, it sends the envelope with `content` inside | Implied by FR-4 | **supported** (W26), and measured: with the jar in reach a `Page<OrderView>` response went from an open shape with zero fields to the envelope's own. Rooting THROUGH `Page` would have published `OrderView` as the body, a contract no client receives |
| The same, without the jar | `Page` and `Slice` live in `spring-data-commons`, not in the module | — | **partially supported.** A module that has not run `extract_libraries` leaves its own `Page<OrderView>` open with zero fields, and `Page` is reported as a type nothing could expand — stated, not silently empty |
| HATEOAS `EntityModel` / `CollectionModel` / `PagedModel` | The same envelope shape one library along | Implied by FR-4 | **unsupported, and unmeasurable here.** These appear in **zero files across all six corpora**, so the rule that covers `Page` has never been exercised on them and claiming they work would be claiming a measurement nobody took. **Falsified by** a corpus that uses one |
| Batch-listener generics | `@KafkaListener(batch = "true")` takes `List<Foo>`, so the message shape is the ELEMENT type and not the list | `docs/roadmap.md`; shipped B/C Task 11 (`kafka-batch-generics`, spec §5a) | **supported.** `spring-kafka.yml` declares a conditional wrapper (`wrappers[].when: { annotation_arg: { annotation: …KafkaListener, name: batch, parses_true: true } }`): where the owning method's own `batch` attribute parses true (Spring's `StringUtils.hasText` then case-insensitive `Boolean.parseBoolean`, so `"true"` and `"TRUE"` both qualify), `List<T>` unwraps at rooting and the row publishes `T`; an ordinary `List<T>` request body with no `batch` attribute at all still publishes the array, unchanged. Pinned by `test_a_batch_true_listener_publishes_the_element_not_the_list` (`tests/application/test_pipeline.py`). **Boundary — still not read:** `spring.kafka.listener.type=batch`, a container-factory property with no annotation trace, so a module relying on it publishes the array (§5.8) |

### 5.15 Configuration source

Where a value comes from, and whether a derived row may read it. The distinction that
matters is not the file format: it is which engine is allowed to ask.

| Source | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| `application.yml` / `.properties`, with profiles | Read into `config_entries` and resolved into `resolved_config`, per environment | FR-19 and D04 | **supported** — both tables ship in the bundle, and an unresolved value carries the reason it stayed unresolved rather than being dropped |
| A derived row reading a resolved key | Two passes do it — `config_join.py` and `derived_resource.py` | D02 | **partially supported.** `annotation_driven` does NOT read `resolved_config`, which is why an Actuator module overriding `management.endpoints.web.base-path` is still published under `/actuator` (§5.1). The boundary is per-ENGINE, not per-key |
| Redaction of a configured value | `redact_value(key, value)`, applied at the exits | FR-19's 2026-09-02 amendment | **supported** — the workspace stays verbatim and the mask happens at the rendered document, the exported bundle, and the enrichment read path |

### 5.16 Trace and diagram

A trace is a named query rather than a table (D01), so this axis is about what the WALK
does and what a diagram drawn from it may assert.

| Surface | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| The walk itself | Root to terminal over `call_edges`, an explicit stack | FR-6, FR-7 | **supported** — see "Where a TRACE stops" below for every reason it halts, each measured |
| Depth bounding | — | FR-6's "not bounded by depth" | **supported** by removal (W31): the bound never fired and is gone, so nothing needs a counter to stay finite |
| Materialised reachability | One row per (root, symbol) with the shallowest depth | F8 §9.4 | **supported** (W37) — §3a-xiii |
| Branch distinction | Two `return` statements are two possible answers; an `if` contributes both sides | — | **unsupported, and ruled rather than missing.** A trace is reachability, never execution. Distinguishing branches would require control flow, which the scanner does not read |
| The contract class diagram | Association, `extends` and `implements` arrows | F7 §8.4 | **partially supported** (W33): `<\|..` implements has **no live instance in any corpus** and ships pinned rather than deferred, with the absence recorded |

### 5.17 Bundle and export

| Artifact | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| Every schema object travels | 26 of 26 tables and views, with no exclusion class | FR-18; D06 §5, ruled by the user (C10) | **supported.** For four phases the export was a hand-written list of eleven names and the seven it omitted — `raw_facts`, `call_edges`, `return_flows`, `roots`, and the views `config_entries`, `field_writes`, `method_returns` — were dropped from every bundle with nothing saying so, which made D06's own sequence-diagram row false as written. A ratchet now asserts the export's query list EQUALS the schema's object list, so the omission cannot recur |
| The versions that produced it | `sextant_version`, `fact_schema_version`, `graph_schema_version` in `manifest.json` | FR-18 | **supported** — D06 §3 |
| Refusing to join bundles of differing vintages | FR-18's second sentence: assembling a system from artifacts of differing vintages is *refused rather than silently joined* | FR-18 | **unsupported, and the recording half is what ships.** The manifest states every version a refusal would need; nothing reads two manifests and compares them, because v1 has no consumer that assembles a system from bundles. The row exists so the half that is missing is visible beside the half that is not |
| The index bundle | Eight NDJSON files an agent greps | F8 §9.3 | **supported** (W35, W36) — §3a-xii |

### 5.18 Question ledger

| Entry | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| `open_questions` | What no programmatic pass could answer, written for the enrichment tier | D06 §5 | **supported** — the table ships in the bundle |
| An overlay that disagrees with the graph | *"No overlay silently overrides evidence: where an overlay and the graph disagree, the disagreement is recorded as an open question"* | FR-15 clause 3 | **unsupported.** `open_questions.py`'s `QUESTIONS` is a CLOSED set of twelve kinds — `record_open_questions` raises on anything outside it — and none of them is an overlay/graph disagreement; its four writers are the format, environment-divergence, service-identity and routing-conflict passes. The overlay FR-15 means is D03's, which has no producer at all (`PROJECT.md` lists D03 and M01's overlay as planned; the 2026-09-05 principles sweep records the whole tier as absent under `B-C2`). The only overlay in `src/` is the ENRICHMENT overlay, a different envelope — and when one of its claims disagrees with the graph, `enrich/validate.py` REFUSES the claim and render excludes it with a count, which is not a ledger entry |
| A curation FILE supplying links and context | *"a claim about the world, never an edit to a fact"* | FR-16 clause 1 | **unsupported.** No curation file exists. The only overlay in `src/` is the ENRICHMENT overlay, which supplies prose rather than links, and D03's overlay tier — the one FR-16 means — has no producer at all (`PROJECT.md` carries D03 and M01 as `planned`; the 2026-09-05 principles sweep records the whole tier absent under `B-C2`) |
| A proposal written in the form a curation takes | *"accepting it requires review rather than transcription"* | FR-16 clause 2 | **unsupported.** There is no curation form to write a proposal in, and no acceptance step: `domain/enrichment.py` carries no proposal or accepted state, and `api.py` loads `.sextant-enrichment.json` and applies it at render unconditionally. `validate.py`'s check is GROUNDING — a claim naming a symbol nothing declares is never written — which is a different guarantee from review |

> **Re-assessed 2026-09-10 (W52 review).** The FR-16 row here read *"no verb asserts, corrects
> or deletes a statement"* as its evidence of a gap. That was evidence against the wording
> **W51 deleted**. The requirement now says curation is *"never an edit to a fact"* — so the
> absence of such a verb is the requirement being honoured, not missed. What is actually
> missing is the curation file and the proposal form, and the two rows above say that instead.

### 5.19 Rendered document

The `## Coverage` section of a rendered module document, and the reporting FR-17 commits to.

| Section | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| The commit a workspace was built from | — | FR-17 clause 1 | **supported** |
| Which source files the compiled artifacts did not account for | `{documents, partial_documents}` | FR-17 clause 2 | **supported**, and an analysis that proceeded despite uncovered sources is recorded as PARTIAL rather than silently complete |
| The distribution of call sites across the resolution values | — | FR-17 clause 3 | **supported** (W38). Every resolution figure this project has ever quoted came from a throwaway script; these are standing. Three distributions ship and the REACH rate leads, because `resolution` alone reads 100% on two of four corpora — it says whether dispatch settled a call, and naming a target the compiler wrote is easy |
| Every capability an extractor lacks that a catalog required | — | FR-17 clause 4 | **supported** — a catalog declares what it `requires` and the workspace says which requirement went unmet |
| Composed on ONE path | `resolve()` and `render` must produce the same summary | — | **supported** (W38): `coverage_of(workspace)` is called by both. Composed only in the resolve pipeline, a figure reached the manifest and never the rendered document |

### 5.20 Query and findings

Four REPORTS about the code's shape, and two command-surface rules — six rows. **The first four are no
longer requirements of Sextant** — W51 moved them out of `REQUIREMENTS.md` to
[Usage scenarios](REQUIREMENTS.md#usage-scenarios), because the user ruled they are things a
CONSUMER derives from what Sextant reports: *"Sextant can only provide the ammo, but won't be
pulling the trigger."* They stay measured here, and the measurement is unchanged — none has a
producer — but `unsupported` now means *we do not build this*, not *we promised this and have
not built it*. The FR numbers are kept as the addresses a reader already has; each is struck
through to say it is retired. Of the two FR-20 rows, the FIFTH is retired too — W51 removed
the clause it measures — and the SIXTH is the live requirement.

| Report | What is committed | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| Drift detection | Where a module holds both a declared contract and an implementation of it, record both and report any disagreement; authority is decided by the BUILD, and an artifact the build does not consume is documentation | ~~FR-11~~ (scenario) | **unsupported.** No pass compares a declared contract to an implementation. The facts a comparison needs — `shapes`, `shape_fields`, and the resources a build consumes — all ship; nothing joins them |
| Refactor findings | Dependency cycles between modules or packages, symbols with disproportionate coupling, violations of a declared layering | ~~FR-12~~ (scenario) | **unsupported.** Note the requirement's own guard, which any producer must honour: a symbol is reported unreachable only where NO unresolved call site exists in the scope searched — and between 22% and 68% of a corpus's graph ends at a boundary, so that guard would withhold most findings on most modules |
| Design findings | Coupling between candidate boundaries, shapes crossing more than one boundary, the contracts each boundary would carry | ~~FR-13~~ (scenario) | **unsupported.** `shapes` and `endpoints` carry what "a shape crossing more than one boundary" needs; no pass asks the question |
| Structural performance risk | Remote hops per endpoint, distinct services involved, an endpoint reached twice in one trace, a persistence or remote call inside a loop, a blocking call on an async path | ~~FR-14~~ (scenario) | **unsupported**, and two of the five are further out than the other three: "inside a loop" and "on an async path" both need control flow, which the scanner does not read (`control_flow` is one of the three unshipped capabilities). The other three are queries over `reachability` and `call_edges` as they stand |
| Approximate subject names | *"where a name is ambiguous Sextant lists the matches and stops rather than choosing"* | ~~FR-20~~ (retired W51) | **unsupported, and the clause is gone.** No command resolves a subject approximately, so the ambiguity rule governed nothing. FR-20's W51 amendment removed it and points here; what a reader wanted is real and ships one layer out, as `<bundle>/index/` |
| Every command takes an exact identifier | *"every command takes one"* — the clause W52 removed from FR-20, kept here as the record of what was promised | FR-20 | **unsupported** (W52), which is why the clause is gone from the requirement rather than left standing. All four verbs take a filesystem PATH that must already exist — `@click.argument("path", type=click.Path(exists=True, …))` in `src/sextant/cli/app.py`, and `sextant.api.analyze(path, *, allow_resolve=False)` has the same shape. Results DO carry exact identifiers; nothing takes one back. FR-20 now states this as its own gap |

### 5.21 Out of scope for v1 (Q3)

Q3 ruled that v1 builds **the five asymmetries only** — the missing half of a surface we
already claim — and that everything else is a stated boundary. These are the two still
open of the four the gap analysis named — Kafka Streams shipped and moved to §5.8, and
Spring Cloud Gateway's Java DSL half shipped (PR A2, `G-30`/`G-30-outbound`) and moved to
§5.1/§5.2 — each `unsupported` with what it would take, so a reader meets them here rather
than inferring them from silence.

| Surface | What it is | Committed at | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Spring Cloud Gateway properties/YAML routes** | Routes declared in `spring.cloud.gateway.server.webflux.routes[0].predicates[0]=Path=/orders/**` rather than the Java DSL | `G-30`, and the design calls the Gateway "the largest single hole" — this is the half PR A2 left standing | **supported** since 2026-09-21 (`gateway-properties-routes`, closed; `spring-cloud-gateway-properties.yml` on `config_join`'s `indexed:` form, CP-29) — the row is in §5.1 and its target rows in §5.2. Kept here because Q3 named it |
| **Programmatic Jakarta WebSocket registration** | `ServerEndpointConfig.Builder.create(Class, "/path")` rather than `@ServerEndpoint` | `G-34` | **unsupported.** `jakarta-websocket.yml` reads the annotation only. The programmatic path is a `single_call` on a builder, and W46 read the CLIENT direction of this same library without touching it |
| **`@DgsEntityFetcher`** | Netflix DGS federation entity resolution | `G-40` | **unsupported.** `netflix-dgs.yml` reads `@DgsQuery`, `@DgsMutation`, `@DgsData` and `@DgsSubscription`; the federation annotation names an entity type rather than a schema field, which is a different identity this catalog has no rule for |

### 5.22 Class-file attributes deliberately not read (F1)

Every attribute JVMS 4.7 defines is in `AttributeLedger` in exactly one of two states — read,
naming the callback that reads it, or deliberately not read with a stated reason. **There is
no third state**, and `AttributeLedgerTest` compares the ledger against JVMS's own list and
refuses a reason that is a deferral in disguise. §2c has the whole argument; this is the
residue, enumerated so it carries a support level like everything else.

| Attribute | Why it is not read | Support |
|---|---|---|
| `StackMapTable` | Verification metadata. It describes no declaration — it tells the verifier what the operand stack looks like at each branch target | **unsupported, and ruled rather than deferred.** Nothing a boundary could be derived from |
| `LocalVariableTypeTable` | Duplicates `Signature` | **unsupported, measured.** It adds a local's generic signature where the plain `LocalVariableTable` has the erased one — and the NAMES W6 wants are in the plain table, while generic parameter types already come from `Signature` (W5). Reading it would duplicate what is read |
| `Module` | Module identity comes from the build's coordinates | **unsupported.** `module-info.class` is suppressed whole |
| `ModulePackages` | The same | **unsupported** |
| `ModuleMainClass` | The same, and it has no visitor to be read from once `module-info.class` is suppressed | **unsupported.** Measured over 1,672 cached jars: 383 carry a module descriptor and 6 of those declare a main class |

### 5.23 Declared slices (Q4)

Q4 ruled that a catalog may cover part of its framework **where the slice is declared** — in
the catalog and here — with what falls outside it. Presence stops meaning completeness. The
cost is a per-catalog enumeration pass, and `scripts/probes/w19_catalog_surface/run.sh` is
what pays it: it reads each owner's declared methods out of the jar and cross-references the
catalog AS LOADED, so a release adding an addressable method fails a test rather than quietly
widening the distance between what a catalog claims and what it reads.

| Slice | Catalog | What is outside | Support |
|---|---|---|---|
| `G-44` | `spring-jms-template`, `spring-rabbit-template`, `spring-sqs-template` | `spring-sqs-template` reads its send half; the receive half now lives in its own sibling catalog, `spring-sqs-template-receive.yml` (`sqs-template-receive`, PR A2 Task 2) — all four `receive*` overloads, the queue named inside each call's own options lambda. `spring-rabbit-template` and `spring-jms-template` also BOTH read both halves (`scripts/probes/w59_template_receive_halves.md`): Rabbit reads `convertAndSend` plus the nine queue-naming `receive`/`receiveAndConvert`/`receiveAndReply` overloads, and JMS reads `convertAndSend` plus the four destination-naming `receive`/`receiveSelected`/`receiveAndConvert`/`receiveSelectedAndConvert` overloads. Each still declares outside: Rabbit's destination-less receive overloads, request/reply forms and setters; JMS's `send`/`sendAndReceive`, `browse`/`browseSelected`, `execute`, the setters, and the `jakarta.jms.Destination`-typed and arity-1-selector overloads of its four read receive names | **partially supported**, declared |
| — | `spring-pubsub` | **NOTHING.** *This file was listed under `G-44` as reading "the send half only" and marked declared; both halves were false.* It declares TEN `direction: inbound` methods — `subscribe`, `subscribeAndConvert`, and the eight `pull*` forms — and derives a live inbound row on the fixture beside its outbound one. It has no `slice:` block because it needs none: the surface probe measures 11 of `PubSubTemplate`'s 20 read and records that **no unread method addresses a wire**, which is the one case the enumeration says needs no slice | ✅ **supported**, both directions |
| `G-37` | `spring-webflux-router`, `spring-mvc-router` | `RouterFunctions$Builder` entirely — 0 of 20 on each side. `route()`'s builder DSL has been the reference documentation's recommended Java shape since Spring 5.2 | **partially supported**, declared |
| `G-43` | `aws-sqs-sdk` | `SqsAsyncClient`'s other 27 methods (`sendMessage` is read on it too, since `G-43`), the batch forms, and the entire inbound direction including `receiveMessage` | **partially supported**, declared |
| `G-45` | `spring-data-rest` | The base contracts plus `JpaRepository` and `MongoRepository` (7 of 28); every other store-specific contract (R2DBC, Cassandra, Elasticsearch, Neo4j, Couchbase, and every reactive/coroutine/RxJava variant) is outside | **partially supported**, declared |
| `G-47` | `spring-integration` | 7 of 11 channel annotations | **partially supported**, declared |
| `G-41` | `netflix-dgs` | **NOT a declared slice, and the row that said so was wrong three ways.** The file is `netflix-dgs.yml`, not `dgs-graphql.yml`; it carries no `slice:` block; and its own comment is headed *"Why this is not `slice.outside`"* — filing it as one *"would let a reader conclude the annotations themselves are unhandled, which is the opposite of true"*. The mechanism this cell used to name is one the catalog records as FALSE: `@Inherited` inherits from SUPERCLASSES and never through methods, and four of the five annotations are `@Target(METHOD)`, so the bit is inert. The real gap is a superclass METHOD walk — `docs/open-issues.md` `OI-67` | ✅ **supported** for the annotations it reads; the inherited-method case is `OI-67`, not a slice |
| — | `spring-stomp-template` | 11 of `SimpMessagingTemplate`'s 14 methods, one of them addressing-shaped | **partially supported** (W43), declared |
